When two syncs share an endpoint but ask for different rates, **the lowest wins** for both — except that `requests_per_second: 0` is the "pacing disabled" *sentinel* rather than a rate, so it never wins the comparison in either direction; one sync opting out must not switch pacing off for every other sync sharing that endpoint. **Vendor ceilings are preserved** and still clamp above whatever is configured — HubSpot 9/s, Zendesk 11/s, GitHub Actions 5/s, Notion 3/s — and are applied to the resolved rate *before* it reaches the registry, so a shared bucket is created already clamped; configuring a lower rate than the ceiling is always honoured. Also new: **`rate_limit.burst`**, an opt-in integer letting idle time accumulate up to N requests' worth of credit spendable back-to-back, for bursty batches that shouldn't raise the sustained rate. `RateLimiter` became **thread-safe** (it had no lock, which was previously safe only because every instance was local to one `load()` call) and `requests_per_second` widened from `int` to `float` (sub-1/s rates like `2.5` were already exercised — a compatibility fix, not a feature). **`burst: null` is byte-identical to previous behaviour**, and a sync with no `rate_limit` anywhere is unchanged. Per-connector docs now state each vendor's published limit alongside the override syntax. Docs: `docs/llm/API_REFERENCE.md`, `docs/connectors/*.md`.

### Added

- **Query tagging for cost attribution** ([#768](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/768)): every query drt issues — read or write — arrived at the warehouse anonymously, so a warehouse admin asking "what does reverse ETL cost us" had no better answer than account-level spend (the exact wall the [#654](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/654) smoke-account cost digest hit). New `query_tagging` block in `drt_project.yml`, on by default (dbt's `query_comment` precedent): `query_tagging: {enabled: true, extra: {team: growth}}`. **Universal fallback**: every query gets a leading `/* drt app=drt sync=<name> run_id=<id> ... */` SQL comment — one dialect-agnostic mechanism baked in at the engine layer, covering every SQL source and destination including the ones with no native tagging concept. **Native mechanisms layer on top** where a warehouse has one: BigQuery `QueryJobConfig(labels=...)`, Snowflake's `QUERY_TAG` session parameter, and Databricks' native `query_tags` connect kwarg — session tags don't retroactively label queries issued before they were set, so the comment stays useful even where a native mechanism exists. Covers both directions: the **extract path** ([#879](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/879) — every `Source.extract()`/`extract_incremental()` call, via an additive keyword-only `query_tags` param rather than smuggling the payload onto the shared `ProfileConfig`, which races under `--threads`) and the **write path** ([#891](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/891) — every INSERT/UPSERT/MERGE/DELETE, via a `TaggedCursor` wrapper around each SQL destination's one `conn.cursor()` seam so the ~20 individual `cur.execute()` call sites per dialect file didn't each need touching; handles both plain-string SQL and `psycopg2.sql.Composable` DDL, and needed its own explicit `__enter__`/`__exit__` since Snowflake/Databricks's `with conn.cursor() as cur:` looks up dunder methods on the type, bypassing instance-level `__getattr__`). `run_id` here is scoped to one sync's single execution, mirroring `started_at`'s existing scope — deliberately not the broader engine-wide run_id/metadata-columns concept tracked by [#762](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/762). ClickHouse's write path gets the comment only — its native `log_comment` setting is a real follow-up outside #768's BigQuery/Snowflake/Databricks scope — and its streaming `client.insert()` has no SQL text to tag at all, documented as a gap rather than silently skipped; BigQuery's streaming `insert_rows_json` has the same gap, since labels are job-scoped and a streaming insert isn't a job. Lookup / `--dry-run --diff` / `drt test` queries are out of scope — a comparably-sized follow-up with a different call shape (they don't currently take a `sync_name`/`run_id` at all). Docs: every connector's `## Notes` section states exactly what is and isn't tagged.

- **`mirror.strategy: tracked` + `mirror.scope` composition, mounted on Snowflake, ClickHouse, and Databricks** ([#692](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/692), composition via [#694](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/694) part 1): closes the two gaps `sync.mode: mirror` had carried since #686/#687 shipped. **Composition**: `strategy: tracked` + `scope` together used to be flatly rejected by config validation ("use one or the other") — now composable everywhere tracked mirror is supported, pruning both the state read and the state rewrite to the scope a run actually observed, so a run touching one parent's children never re-diffs or re-writes another parent's tracked history. `scope` must be a subset of `destination.upsert_key`: scope values are derived positionally from the already-tracked key rather than stored in a new state-table column, since `_drt_synced_keys` is only ever `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` with no migration path anywhere in this code — a required new column would silently never materialise on an already-deployed table. **Dialect mount**: every SQL destination that implements `sync.mode: mirror` at all now supports both `strategy: tracked` and `scope` — Postgres/MySQL (already shipped), **Snowflake** (own `SHOW TABLES LIKE ... IN SCHEMA` existence probe, reusing the replace-swap path's established pattern since Snowflake has no `to_regclass`-cheap equivalent), **ClickHouse** (deletes are `ALTER TABLE ... DELETE` mutations, not a plain `DELETE`, on both the destination- and tracked-strategy paths; no cross-statement transaction, so the target delete deliberately runs *before* the state rewrite — a target-delete failure degrades safely into next run's baseline WARN, whereas the reverse order would leave forgotten target rows with nothing left to ever clean them up), and **Databricks** (reused the existing staging+anti-join delete helper, since `databricks-sql-connector`'s native `?` paramstyle caps statements at 255 parameter markers — a large inline `IN`/`NOT IN` list was never an option here). BigQuery has no mirror mode at all and stays out of scope. A shared `check_scope_subset_of_upsert_key` and a `supports_tracked_scope` opt-in flag on `check_mirror_supported` keep the constraint and its rejection wording from drifting as each dialect landed in its own PR. Docs: `docs/connectors/{postgres,snowflake,clickhouse,databricks}.md`.

- **Tracked mirror's state diff moved from Python to SQL** ([#694](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/694) part 2, **closes #694**): the state-diff read used to `SELECT` every key a sync had ever tracked into Python and diff it against the current run with a Python `set` — fine at hundreds of keys, but a state table with millions of rows got fully read into memory just to compute a diff that's typically tiny (most runs only change a handful of rows). Now the current run's keys are staged into a scratch table and the database computes `previous - current` via a `NOT EXISTS` join against `_drt_synced_keys`, so only the (small) diff — and the genuinely-new keys to insert — ever reach Python. Landed across all four dialects that support tracked mirror: **Postgres/MySQL** (`CREATE TEMPORARY TABLE` staging, the one spelling both accept), **Snowflake** (session-scoped `TEMPORARY TABLE`), **ClickHouse** (no staging table at all — `Array(String)` named parameters already hold the whole current-key-hash set as one bound value, so both diff directions are plain `NOT IN` / `arrayJoin` queries against that parameter instead of a joined-against table), and **Databricks** (a second scratch table alongside the existing target-delete staging table, chunked through `_rows_per_chunk` for the native `?` paramstyle's 255-marker limit). The old "read every untouched row so it can be reinserted unchanged" step for scope-preserved rows is gone on every dialect — untouched rows are simply never selected by either the diff query or the new-keys query, which has the same net effect as reading and reinserting them unchanged, without ever having to. ⚠️ **This memory win is unscoped-only.** The diff query filters by `sync_name` alone, not by scope — scope-filtering happens in Python *after* the SQL-side diff. The result is still correct (scope membership and current-run membership are independent conditions, so filtering the diff by scope afterward is equivalent to filtering the full previous set by scope first), but a run touching one scope out of many historically-tracked ones gets a diff close to the *entire* previous state, not a small one — tracked as [#890](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/890) for a real fix (needs dialect-specific JSON extraction from `key_json`, since scope isn't a separate state-table column, or reopening the schema-migration tradeoff #694 part 1 deliberately avoided). **Databricks-specific race condition caught in independent review before merge**: the new diff scratch table wasn't disambiguated by target table the way its sibling `keys_table` already was — since Databricks has no session-local temp tables, this was a real shared, persistent object per `catalog.schema`, and two tracked-mirror syncs targeting different tables in the same schema running concurrently under `drt run --threads N>1` (a documented, supported feature) would race on it, one thread's `CREATE OR REPLACE`/`INSERT` clobbering the other's mid-flight and corrupting the diff for both. Fixed by suffixing the scratch table name with `config.table`, matching the existing `keys_table` pattern. Docs: `docs/connectors/postgres.md`.

## [0.8.3] - 2026-07-27

**Theme: seeing what a run will do — and what it quietly did.** `drt run --dry-run --diff` previewed record-level upserts but not the **mirror `DELETE` pass** ([#693](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/693)) — so the destructive half of a `mode: mirror` sync was invisible until it ran. All three mirror strategies are now previewed read-only, labelled distinctly from a replace-mode rebuild, and the same preview got **cheaper**: the destination lookup batches by source primary keys instead of scanning the whole table ([#470](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/470)). On the "what did it quietly do" side, **`alerts.on_degraded`** ([#784](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/784)) puts thresholds on the failure modes that *succeed* — a creeping error rate, an SLA breach, an empty source, a growing DLQ — and **`drt test`** gains custom SQL `query` tests, `severity: warn`, and `--store-failures` to write the offending rows to disk ([#779](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/779), masked before writing). Internally, the Postgres/MySQL destinations shed their duplicated orchestration into a shared base ([#719](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/719), closed) and the MCP server, `schema.py`, and `run.py` were split along the same lines ([#723](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/723)). No breaking changes — drop-in upgrade from v0.8.2.

### Added

- **Custom SQL query tests, `severity: warn`, and `drt test --store-failures`** ([#779](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/779)): `drt test` gains a `query` test type — arbitrary SQL that returns the *failing* rows (`{{ table }}` renders to the qualified destination table; zero rows = pass) — for assertions the five fixed types (`row_count` / `not_null` / `freshness` / `unique` / `accepted_values`) can't express. Every test type gains `severity: warn | error` (default `error`, so existing suites are unchanged): a `warn` failure is still run, reported, and counted, but never fails the exit code — `--output json` lists every warn-severity failure under a top-level `warnings` array. `drt test --store-failures` writes up to N failing rows per failed test to `.drt/test_failures/<sync>/<test>.jsonl` (`--store-failures-limit`, default 10) — a snapshot of the current run (overwritten each time; a now-passing test's stale sample is removed) with `sync.mask` ([#427](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/427)) applied *before* anything is written, so masked columns stay masked in the failure file. `row_count` has no per-row failure concept, so `--store-failures` is a no-op for it. An optional `name:` (recommended for `query`, usable on any type) labels a test and names its failure-sample file. `test_display_name()` — the map #400 was a drift bug in — now covers `query` and is pinned by a coverage test. `drt build` and the `drt_run_test` MCP tool share the same severity/query logic via the existing shared test-execution seam, so neither drifts from `drt test`.

- **Alert conditions — degraded-sync thresholds (`alerts.on_degraded`)** ([#784](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/784)): `alerts.on_failure` ([#414](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/414)) fires on hard failure only, but real reverse-ETL degradation is partial — a creeping error rate, a duration SLA breach, an empty source that "succeeds", a DLQ backlog accumulating across runs. A new `on_degraded:` block adds thresholds on four post-sync metrics, evaluated from data already in `SyncResult` + the DLQ store (no new collection): `row_errors_pct` (`failed / rows_extracted`; `skipped` is excluded since it's a normal outcome via #757 match_policy / `--limit` / `lookups.check_only`; 0% when nothing was extracted), `duration_seconds`, `rows_extracted` (`{eq: 0}` empty-source guard), and `dlq_depth`. Conditions are a **mapping** metric → threshold (`{gt/lt/gte/lte/eq: N}`, exactly one operator); multiple trips **coalesce into one message per sync per run**; `--output json` gains `conditions_tripped`. `on_degraded.channels` reuses the existing Slack/webhook dispatch and defaults to empty, so conditions can be JSON-only (for CI) without a paging target. Evaluated at the CLI seam (`_run_one`), so the engine is untouched and `drt build` inherits it. `on_failure` is unchanged. Docs: `docs/llm/API_REFERENCE.md`.

- **`--diff` previews mirror deletions** ([#693](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/693)): `drt run --dry-run --diff` used to show `deleted` for `mode: replace` only, so a `mode: mirror` sync — the one mode that removes rows from a table the destination otherwise keeps — previewed as adds and updates with the deletions invisible. **All mirror strategies are now previewed, read-only.** `mirror.strategy: tracked` reads drt's own `_drt_synced_keys` state and reports `previous − current`; `strategy: destination` (the [#340](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/340) default) reads the destination's `upsert_key` set and reports `destination − source`, which is the complement the keyed lookup ([#470](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/470)) structurally cannot see. `mirror.scope` ([#687](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/687)) narrows the preview exactly as it narrows the real `DELETE`: the scope values the source records would produce are recomputed for the dry run (the real path accumulates them during `load()`, which a dry run never calls) and filter the key read **server-side**, using the same clause shape each dialect's `_build_mirror_delete` emits — so the preview and the run select the same rows. An **empty source previews no deletions**, matching the real run's early return that keeps a transient empty source from wiping the destination. Entirely **read-only**: no state is written, no state table is created, no `DELETE` runs, and a failed read (missing state table, denied `SELECT`, or a dialect whose mirror pass is its own — ClickHouse / Snowflake) degrades to "nothing to preview" rather than failing the diff. Because a mirror `DELETE` is a different kind of change than a replace-mode rebuild, it is **labelled distinctly** rather than folded into the existing count: text renders `- Deleted (N, mirror DELETE): (key columns only)`, and the `destination` strategy adds `, read from destination` because it is the one strategy whose preview costs an extra destination round trip — a cost a shared label would hide. `--output json` gains a sibling `delete_reason` key (`"replace"` | `"mirror"` | `"mirror_scan"` | `null`; the two mirror values share a blast radius and differ in preview cost). Preview rows carry the `upsert_key` columns only, since both previews read keys rather than full rows. Replace-mode output is **byte-identical** to prior releases and the `deleted` JSON key keeps its shape, so existing consumers are unaffected. Docs: `docs/guides/dry-run-and-diff.md`.

### Changed

- **Diff perf — keyed destination lookup** ([#470](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/470)): `drt run --dry-run --diff` now batches the destination existence lookup by source primary keys (`WHERE <key> IN (…)`, parameterized, bounded batches) instead of reading the whole destination table, avoiding a full-table scan on large destinations. Applies when an `upsert_key` is set and `sync.mode != replace`; **replace mode** (needs the source-key complement for `deleted`), **non-queryable** destinations, **ClickHouse** (different paramstyle), and **no-`upsert_key`** configs fall back to the prior full-scan / sample behavior — the add/update/delete result is unchanged.
- **Internal — Postgres/MySQL destination load-path dedup** ([#719](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/719)): `BaseSqlDestination` now owns the dialect-agnostic orchestration that was duplicated byte-for-byte across the two SQL destinations. **Phase 1:** a dialect-hook surface (`_dialect_connect` / `_qualify_ident`), the shared `test_connection`, and the per-row failure-recording block (`_record_row_error`, the [#722](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/722) seam) lifted out of all six `_load_replace` / `_load_replace_swap` / `_load_upsert` paths. **Phase 2a:** the `load` template method (mode dispatch → `_load_*` → `_accumulate_mirror_state` → `finalize_sync`) and `finalize_sync`'s swap-rename dispatch, with the dialect rename DDL behind a `_rename_swap` hook (PG: two `ALTER TABLE … RENAME` under one commit; MySQL: one atomic `RENAME TABLE`) and shadow/old naming behind `_shadow_name` / `_old_name`. **Phase 2b:** the `sync.mode: mirror` end-of-sync DELETE pass — both `_finalize_mirror` ([#340](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/340) / [#687](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/687)) and `_finalize_mirror_tracked` ([#686](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/686)) — with the irreducible dialect differences behind four hooks: `_build_mirror_delete` (the placeholder-expansion seam — psycopg2 auto-expands a tuple against one `%s`, pymysql needs an explicit `%s` list and flattened params; shared by both paths via a `negate` flag), plus `_state_table_ident` / `_state_table_exists` (`to_regclass` vs `information_schema`) / `_create_state_table` for the `_drt_synced_keys` bookkeeping. Success counting, `match_policy` skip detection, dialect error recovery, and all wire SQL / parameter shapes stay in the subclasses. No behavior change — every existing mirror-mode test, which asserts on the exact statement text and parameter structure, passes unmodified. Snowflake/Databricks follow in #720.
- **Internal — MCP tools split** ([#723](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/723) part 1): `drt/mcp/server.py`'s 14 `@mcp.tool()` closures each re-loaded project/sync config inline. Extracted into `drt/mcp/tools/` (one module per tool, independently testable without a running server) plus a shared `McpContext` (`drt/mcp/_context.py`, `_load_ctx(project_dir)`) that centralizes the `load_project` / `load_syncs` / `load_syncs_safe` / find-sync-by-name calls that were duplicated across tools. `server.py` now only builds the context and registers each tool via a thin wrapper that keeps the exact original name, parameters, and docstring MCP clients see; the wrapper body is a one-line delegate into `tools/`. No behavior change — every tool's public signature/docstring is byte-identical (verified via AST diff), and all 55 tests exercising `create_server`/the MCP tools pass unmodified. `schema.py` table-driving, `_connect()` normalization, and `run.py` cleanup remain for future PRs.
- **Internal — `schema.py` table-driven introspection** ([#723](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/723) part 2): `describe_columns`'s four nearly-identical `_describe_{postgres,mysql,snowflake,databricks}` implementations (connect → query → fetch → categorize, each repeated) are now one shared `_describe` pipeline plus a `_DESCRIBE_SPECS` table (keyed by `config.type`, matching `drt/connectors/registry.py`'s convention) holding only what genuinely differs per dialect: the SQL/params, the `connect` call (staticmethod vs. instance method — unchanged, `_connect()` normalization is a separate future PR), whether the cursor is used as a context manager, and the `data_type` → category mapping. No behavior change — public API (`describe_columns`, `describe_databricks_ddls`) is byte-identical (verified via AST diff), and all 22 `test_schema.py` tests plus every other test touching this module pass unmodified.
- **Internal — destination `_connect()` normalization** ([#723](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/723) part 3): Postgres/MySQL/ClickHouse exposed `_connect` as `@staticmethod`; Snowflake/Databricks as a plain instance method, forcing external callers (`query.py`, `schema.py`) to instantiate just to call it (`SnowflakeDestination()._connect(config)`). All five are now `@classmethod` — callable both ways, so every existing internal `self._connect(config)` call site needed no change; the 6 external `X()._connect(config)` call sites were simplified to `X._connect(config)`. No behavior change — `@patch`-based test mocks target the class attribute regardless of descriptor type, so no test needed updating; 474 relevant tests pass unmodified.
- **Internal — `cli/commands/run.py` cleanup** ([#723](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/723) part 4, final): the last remaining #723 item (JSON logging was already extracted in #729). `_exit_code_for_signal` (the generic POSIX 128 + signum mapping) moved to `drt/cli/_helpers.py`, the existing shared-CLI-utility module; `run.py` re-imports it under its original name, same pattern already used for `_configure_json_logging`. `_run_one`'s observer-composition block (building the `LoggingObserver` / `StatePersistingObserver` / opt-in `DlqObserver` stack into one `CompositeObserver`) is now `_build_observer`, a same-file helper. No behavior change — both extractions are verbatim moves; 550 relevant tests pass unmodified.

## [0.8.2] - 2026-07-21

**Theme: navigable docs hosting on authenticated object stores.** A focused follow-up completing v0.8.1's `--inline` ([#818](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/818)): that release made each `drt docs` page self-contained (fixing *styling* on a GCS / S3 authenticated-object URL) but left the site multi-object, so **navigation still dead-ended** — a per-object authenticated viewer isn't a web server, and the relative inter-object links break. `drt docs generate --inline` now emits the **entire catalog as one navigable HTML object** (the Elementary single-file model): overview, DAG, and every sync / source / destination / tag become `<section>`s with in-page `#hash` navigation, so it both renders *and* navigates from a single authenticated object URL — zero sub-resource and zero inter-object requests. Per-page display is byte-identical to the default multi-file output, which is unchanged. No breaking changes — drop-in upgrade from v0.8.1.

### Changed

- **`drt docs generate --inline` now emits a true single-object bundle** ([#821](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/821), completing [#818](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/818)): v0.8.1's `--inline` inlined CSS/JS into every page but left the site **multi-object** (`index.html` + `sync/*.html` + …). That fixed *styling* on an authenticated object store (GCS `storage.cloud.google.com`, S3 presigned URLs), but **navigation still broke** — a `storage.cloud.google.com/<bucket>/<object>` URL is a per-object authenticated viewer, not a web server, so the landing page rendered while every relative inter-object link (a per-sync page, the DAG) dead-ended. `--inline` now assembles the **entire catalog** — overview, DAG, and every sync / source / destination / tag — into **one navigable HTML object**: each page becomes a `<section>`, inter-page links are rewritten to in-page `#hash` anchors, and a tiny router shows one section at a time (default overview). This is the Elementary single-file model — the only shape that both renders *and* navigates on an authenticated object store without a web server. **Zero sub-resource and zero inter-object requests.** Per-page **display is byte-identical** to the default multi-file output (verified section-by-section); it degrades gracefully with JS off (all sections render stacked, `#hash` links jump natively); and it stays byte-identical across regenerations ([#697](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/697)). The default multi-file output is unchanged (opt-in). The rmtree regeneration guard now keys on `index.html` + `manifest.json` (both emitted in every mode). Note: since v0.8.1 `--inline` shipped only hours earlier and never delivered navigable hosting, this completes the flag's promise rather than breaking a relied-upon shape.

## [0.8.1] - 2026-07-21

**Theme: reverse-ETL activation semantics + docs hosting + hardening.** A follow-up release on the v0.8.0 line. **`sync.match_policy`** brings Census/Hightouch-style `update_only` / `create_only` to the upsert write path ([#757](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/757)) — push warehouse-computed fields into records a CRM already has, without creating junk rows — shipping on **Postgres and HubSpot**, with match-policy skips named via a new `SyncResult.skipped_no_match` counter. **`drt docs generate --inline`** ([#818](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/818)) emits self-contained HTML so the catalog can be hosted on an authenticated object store (GCS / S3) where relative asset fetches 401. **Manifest schema v2** ([#698](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/698)) carries run history, declared column facts, and DLQ depth. Plus hardening: tracked mirror pre-provisions its state table without a `CREATE` grant ([#695](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/695)), and HTTP retries honour `Retry-After` ([#769](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/769)). No breaking changes — drop-in upgrade from v0.8.0.

### Added

- **`drt docs generate --inline` — self-contained HTML for authenticated object stores** ([#818](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/818)): the default `--format html` site is multi-file — every page links shared `assets/{style.css,pygments-default.css,app.js}` via **relative** paths. That renders fine from `file://` or a plain web server, but on an **authenticated object store** (GCS `storage.cloud.google.com` links, S3 presigned URLs) the top-level `index.html` authorizes and renders while each relative `assets/*` sub-resource request 401/403s — so the catalog loads completely unstyled. This blocks the common "host the catalog on a private GCS/S3 bucket, share the authenticated link" workflow (the way teams host Elementary / dbt docs). `--inline` embeds the CSS/JS into **every** page (`<link>`/`<script src>` → `<style>`/`<script>`) and omits the `assets/` directory, so each page renders with **zero sub-resource requests** (the favicon is already a `data:` URI). Output stays **multi-page** — inter-page navigation still works because each target page is itself self-contained — and remains **byte-identical across regenerations** ([#697](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/697)); the default multi-file output is unchanged (opt-in). The rmtree regeneration guard now recognises a prior build by `index.html` + `dag.html` (both emitted in every mode) rather than `index.html` + `assets/`, so an inline site re-generates cleanly. Prior art: Elementary's single-file `elementary_report.html`. Docs: `docs/llm/CONTEXT.md` + CLI `--help`.

- **Manifest schema v2 — run history, declared column facts, and DLQ depth per sync** ([#698](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/698)): the `drt docs` manifest now carries the facts the site's run views and badges need (ADR 0003). Per sync: `runs` — up to `--history-depth` recent executions from `.drt/history` (newest first, default 10, duration/status/record counts/errors; new `history_depth` param on the MCP `drt_get_manifest` tool for parity), `fields` — declared write-side column facts from `sync.field_mappings` + `sync.mask` (the column-level-lineage source, [#808](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/808); declared columns only, no SQL parsing), and `dlq_depth` — the sync's current Dead Letter Queue depth. `runs`/`dlq_depth` are machine-local and ride the existing `--no-state` switch; `fields` derives from the repo and is always emitted. v2 is a pure superset of v1 — nothing renamed or removed, no migration needed. Also closes the #698 interlock with [#696](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/696): embedded error text (`state.last_error`, previously shipped verbatim, and `runs[].errors`) is now redacted by default — URLs/DSNs, hosts, e-mails, phone numbers, and credential-ish `key=value` fragments are masked unless `--full-labels`.

- **`sync.match_policy` — `update_only` / `create_only` for the upsert write path** ([#757](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/757)): reverse-ETL activation frequently wants only *one* side of an upsert. `match_policy: update_only` touches only rows that already exist in the destination — the enrichment case, pushing warehouse-computed scores into CRM contacts sales already created, without spraying a junk contact for every warehouse row. `create_only` inserts only rows that don't yet exist — seed an audience once, never overwrite fields a rep has since hand-edited. No-match / already-exists rows are **skipped** (counted in `SyncResult.skipped`, surfaced as `… N skipped` in `drt run`), never errors. The default stays `upsert` (insert-and-update), so existing syncs are unchanged. Applies to the upsert-family modes (`full` / `upsert` / `incremental`); **rejected at validate time for `mode: replace`** (TRUNCATE makes only-update / only-create meaningless) **and `mode: mirror`** (its delete pass is a separate design), and a destination that doesn't implement the policy **fails fast** via a new structural `MatchPolicyCapable` capability check in the engine rather than silently upserting. Ships on the **Postgres** destination first — `create_only` → `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (key) DO NOTHING`, `update_only` → `UPDATE ... SET <non-key> WHERE <key>`, both using Postgres's unambiguous `rowcount` (inserted / matched) as the exact skip signal. MySQL is deliberately deferred: its `UPDATE` reports *changed* rows, not *matched* rows, and interacts with `ON DUPLICATE KEY`, so a correct skip count needs its own careful PR — the same "one destination per PR on a shared engine seam" track the issue anticipates for the SaaS/CRM legs (HubSpot / Salesforce / Intercom) where `update_only` is most valuable. Prior art: Census / Hightouch "Update Only" / "Create Only" sync behaviours (dbt/dlt have no equivalent — this is reverse-ETL-specific). Docs: `docs/connectors/postgres.md` + `docs/llm/API_REFERENCE.md`.

- **`match_policy` — HubSpot leg** ([#757](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/757)): the SaaS/CRM destination where `update_only` matters most now honours the policy. HubSpot's v3 Objects API makes create-vs-update a status code, so skips are detected with no extra lookup: `update_only` PATCHes by `id_property` directly and a **404 (no such record) is skipped, never created** — push warehouse-computed scores into contacts reps already made without spraying junk contacts; `create_only` POSTs only and a **409 (already exists) is skipped, never overwritten**. Default `upsert` (POST → PATCH on 409) is unchanged. Skips land in `SyncResult.skipped` / `skipped_no_match`. Implements `supported_match_policies()` so the engine's `MatchPolicyCapable` guard accepts the policy on HubSpot. First SaaS leg on the Postgres-established seam; Salesforce / Intercom follow the same shape. Docs: `docs/connectors/hubspot.md`.

### Changed

- **`match_policy` skips are named — `SyncResult.skipped_no_match`** ([#757](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/757)): `update_only` / `create_only` (#757) count a row the destination declined (no update target / already exists) in `SyncResult.skipped`, but that bucket also holds `lookups.check_only`, `sync.mask`, and `--limit` skips — so operators couldn't tell an expected "no CRM match" from a dropped row. A new `skipped_no_match` counter is a **named subset of `skipped`** (rows are still counted in `skipped`, so `total` and every existing consumer are unchanged) that isolates match-policy skips. `drt run` now prints `… N skipped (M no match)`; `--output json` gains `rows_skipped` (previously absent from the per-sync entry) and, when non-zero, `rows_skipped_no_match`; the engine emits a `load.skipped_no_match` span attribute. The Postgres leg (and every future `match_policy` destination) populates it. Purely additive — no behaviour change for syncs that don't use `match_policy`.

- **Tracked mirror pre-provisions its state table without a `CREATE` grant** ([#695](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/695)): `mirror.strategy: tracked` (#686) lazily creates a `_drt_synced_keys` bookkeeping table in the destination, which required the sync user to hold `CREATE`. That defeats the canonical least-privilege reverse-ETL setup — a user granted exactly `SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE(/DELETE)` on the target and nothing else — and on **MySQL** it fails in a particularly nasty way: `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` checks the `CREATE` privilege *before* the existence check, so even pre-creating the table by hand did not avoid the grant (`(1142, "CREATE command denied … for table '_drt_synced_keys'")`). The tracked-mirror finalize pass now **probes for the state table first** (`to_regclass()` on Postgres, `information_schema.tables` on MySQL) and only issues the `CREATE` when it is genuinely absent. An admin can therefore pre-provision `_drt_synced_keys` once and run the sync user with **no DDL privilege at all** — the sanctioned pattern for "no `CREATE` for application users" environments — while the lazy-create default is unchanged for users who do hold `CREATE`. The connector docs ([`postgres.md`](docs/connectors/postgres.md) / [`mysql.md`](docs/connectors/mysql.md)) gain a "Required destination privileges" note enumerating the exact grants and the two traps this closes (the delayed `DELETE`-grant failure; `IF NOT EXISTS` still needing `CREATE`).

- **HTTP retries honour the `Retry-After` header** ([#769](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/769)): on a retryable response (429 / 503), `with_retry` previously slept its own blind exponential backoff even when the API said exactly how long to wait — retrying too early (burning attempts against Slack / HubSpot / Intercom / Zendesk, which all send the header) or, on a smaller max, too late. It now parses `Retry-After` in both RFC 7231 forms (delay-seconds and HTTP-date) and sleeps `max(retry_after, computed_backoff)`, still bounded by `max_backoff` so a large or hostile header can't stall the run. Network failures and header-less responses keep the exact prior exponential-backoff behaviour, so nothing changes for destinations that don't send the header. Extraction is defensive (a response object without a usable `headers` mapping degrades to backoff rather than raising inside the retry loop). This is the reliability half of the rate-limiting-v2 issue; the per-destination `rate_limit` override and process-wide shared bucket across `--threads` ([#769](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/769) scope items 2–3) remain as follow-ups. Prior art: dlt's HTTP client honours `Retry-After`. Docs: `docs/llm/API_REFERENCE.md`.

## [0.8.0] - 2026-07-17

**Theme: the `drt docs` site + a developer-experience pull-in wave.** Most of the original v0.8 connector scope shipped early on the v0.7.x line, so v0.8.0 is defined by two things. First, the **`drt docs` site reaches its designed form** — the epic's release gate: a static, zero-runtime-dependency HTML catalog with an ownership-zone lineage DAG (static SVG, byte-identical across regenerations), and **security-hardened by default** — destination labels and node ids no longer leak endpoints, phone numbers, emails, or bucket names into a hosted site ([#696](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/696), brute-force-audited in review). Second, a wave of **dbt/dlt-familiar DX**: project `vars:` + `{{ var('name') }}` + `--vars` ([#783](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/783)), `drt build` (run + test in one pass, [#777](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/777)), `--fail-fast` / `--limit` / `--failed` on `drt run` ([#775](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/775)/[#774](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/774)/[#773](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/773)), dbt-style selection v2 (globs, `--exclude`, `destination:`, [#771](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/771)), incremental REST API extraction ([#767](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/767)), late-arrival `watermark.lag` ([#759](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/759)), and `drt deploy github-actions` scaffolding ([#785](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/785)). Config internals were split for maintainability (`models.py` → destination/sync-option modules, profiles → `profiles.py`, [#721](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/721)) with the public import surface unchanged. No breaking changes — drop-in upgrade from v0.7.11.

### Added

- **Project vars — `vars:` block, `--vars` override, `var()` in model SQL and YAML** ([#783](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/783)): anything project-shaped (a default lookback window, a campaign tag, the pipeline id that differs between sandbox and prod) gets a reviewed default in the repo instead of an environment variable documented per runner — completing the parameterization layer `${ENV}` substitution ([#385](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/385)/[#240](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/240)) started. Declare `vars:` in `drt_project.yml`, reference `{{ var('name') }}` / `{{ var('name', default) }}` in model SQL and YAML string fields, and override per run with `drt run --vars 'lookback_days: 1, pipeline: sandbox'`. Precedence: `--vars` > `DRT_VAR_<NAME>` env > project `vars:`. Passing no vars means "resolve from the project" (the contract `load_syncs` already had), so any command or library caller that runs a sync gets the `vars:` block + `DRT_VAR_*` without threading them — only `--vars` needs explicit plumbing. An undefined var with no default is a **validate-time** error (`drt validate` reports it per file) rather than a mid-run failure — including vars used inside `model:` SQL. `var()` joins the deliberately tiny SQL template surface (`cursor_value` / `watermark`) and composes with it in one render; full Jinja control flow/macros remain an explicit non-goal. Var values interpolate into SQL text exactly like `${ENV}` does — they are reviewed project config, not user input; `drt run` warns (never blocks) when a var value carries SQL metacharacters, which usually means a quoting mistake. Prior art: dbt's `vars:` + `--vars`, dlt's layered config. Docs: `docs/llm/API_REFERENCE.md` + `CONTEXT.md`.

- **`sync.watermark.lag` — overlap window for late-arriving rows** ([#759](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/759)): incremental syncs can widen the *read* window behind the stored watermark so rows that arrive late (upstream backfills, clock skew, rebuilt marts) are re-synced instead of stranded — `WHERE cursor > (watermark - lag)`. Timestamp cursors take a duration string (`lag: "1 hour"`, same grammar as `freshness.max_age`); numeric cursors take a positive int. Applies **only to storage-sourced watermarks** — `--cursor-value` overrides stay exact and `default_value` first runs already mark a user-chosen start — and the *persisted* watermark is never lagged (`new_cursor_value` seeds from the unlagged value), so the window can never regress on an empty run. The lag-adjusted cursor is what `SyncResult.cursor_value_used` reports, a new `watermark_lag` field lands in `--output json` entries, the post-run summary prints an overlap note, and the observer emits a `storage_lag` watermark-resolved event. Duration parsing moves to a shared `drt/config/duration.py` (mirrors the `freshness.max_age` grammar). Prior art: dlt's incremental `lag`/attribution window. Overlap rows are re-sent every run, so destinations must tolerate duplicates (e.g. `upsert_key`). Docs: `docs/llm/API_REFERENCE.md` + `CONTEXT.md`.

- **REST API source — incremental extraction (`incremental.start_param`)** ([#767](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/767)): `mode: incremental` now works end-to-end with the REST API source. A new optional `IncrementalSource` capability Protocol (same pattern as `ConnectionTestable` / `StagedDestination`) lets the engine hand the resolved watermark straight to API-shaped sources — SQL sources keep consuming it through the rendered `WHERE` clause, but REST has no SQL to carry it, so `RestApiSource.extract()` previously ignored the cursor entirely and re-pulled the full endpoint every run. With `incremental: {start_param: updated_since}` on the `rest_api` profile, drt injects the last watermark as a request query param (`GET <url>?updated_since=<watermark>`) so the API filters server-side. Injection composes with all three pagination styles — every page for `offset`/`cursor`/no-pagination, first request only for `link_header` (the server's `next` links are authoritative). Engine-side cursor tracking, `sync.watermark` storage/`default_value`, and `--cursor-value` all compose unchanged. `mode: incremental` without `start_param` keeps the old full-extraction behaviour and logs a warning. Prior art: dlt's `rest_api` incremental. Docs: REST API source section added to `docs/connectors/rest-api.md` (the source half was previously undocumented) + llm docs.

- **`drt deploy github-actions` — scaffold a scheduled sync workflow with required secrets enumerated** ([#785](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/785)): generates `.github/workflows/drt-sync.yml` wired to `drt-hub/drt-action@v1` from the project root. The error-prone part is automated: connector **extras are inferred** from `profiles.yml` + sync destination types, and **every required secret** — each `*_env` reference and `${VAR}` placeholder across syncs/profiles — lands in the step's `env:` block as `NAME: ${{ secrets.NAME }}`, with a printed `gh secret set NAME` checklist. `--schedule "40 3 * * *"` adds the cron trigger (omit for manual dispatch with a commented example); `--select` / `--profile` / `--extras` override inference; `--dry-run` previews; `--force` overwrites. The scanner reads YAML raw (no `${VAR}` expansion), so it works in a fresh checkout with no runtime env vars set. Two drift-guard tests pin the type→extra mapping to the connector registry and pyproject extras. Prior art: `dlt deploy github-action`. Docs: CI/CD guide + README/llm CLI references.

- **`drt build` — run each sync and its tests in one invocation** ([#777](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/777)): the `dbt build` muscle-memory command. Per sync: run → on success, immediately execute its `tests:` (non-queryable destinations skip tests exactly like `drt test`; a failed run skips its now-meaningless assertions). A failing test marks the sync **failed** in the exit code and `--output json` (`status: "tests_failed"`, per-sync `tests` array nested in each entry) — already-loaded data is *not* rolled back, same as dbt. Composes with selection v2 (`--select`/`--exclude`), `--fail-fast`, `--dry-run` (run preview + test plan), `--profile`, `--quiet`. The per-sync test execution was extracted from `drt test` into a shared `execute_tests_for_sync()` so both commands run identical logic. v1 is sequential by design — `--threads` interleaving needs the run-loop extraction (#723 family); use `drt run --threads` + `drt test` when parallelism matters.

- **`--fail-fast` on `drt run` and `drt test` — stop scheduling after the first failure** ([#775](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/775)): when one sync fails, don't burn minutes and API quota running the rest of a red build — and when the failure is systemic (expired credential, warehouse down), page once instead of N times. Sequential mode stops scheduling immediately; `--threads` mode cancels not-yet-started syncs while **in-flight syncs drain normally** (reusing the executor-shutdown semantics rather than hard-cancelling mid-load, consistent with #279's graceful-shutdown philosophy). Skipped syncs get a first-class `status: "skipped"` + `reason: "fail_fast"` in `--output json` (a new top-level `skipped` count joins `succeeded`/`failed`), so tooling can tell "didn't run" from "ran and failed". `drt test --fail-fast` stops after the first sync with a failing test and reports the remaining syncs skipped. Exit codes unchanged (non-zero on failure).

- **`drt run --limit N` — sampled runs for safe first sends** ([#774](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/774)): extract at most N rows per sync, then *really* load them — the "send 10 rows, check them in the destination UI, then open the tap" onboarding move (Census/Hightouch "test with sample records"; dlt `add_limit`). The cap is applied engine-side (`itertools.islice` on the extraction iterator) so it works identically for SQL, REST, and file sources with no per-dialect LIMIT/TOP rendering. Safety rails: **watermarks never advance** on a sampled run (the skipped rows would otherwise fall permanently behind the cursor), `SyncResult.limit_applied` + a `limit` field in `--output json` mark the run as sampled, and `--limit` is **refused for `mode: mirror` / `replace`** syncs — a sampled mirror would delete the destination rows the sample skipped. Composes with `--dry-run --diff` for a capped no-send preview.

- **`drt run --failed` — sync-level re-run of the previous invocation's failures** ([#773](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/773)): re-runs only syncs whose last recorded status in `.drt/state.json` is not `success` (`failed` *and* `partial`; never-run syncs are not included). Intersects with `--select`/`--exclude` (selection first, then the failed filter). When the previous state is clean it prints a note and **exits 0** — recovery loops shouldn't page when there is nothing to recover (JSON mode emits `"note": "nothing_failed"`). Failed incremental syncs never advanced their watermark, so re-runs are naturally correct. Complementary to `drt retry` (record-level DLQ replay, #278) — this is the dbt-retry-shaped sync-level counterpart. `drt test --failed` from the issue is deferred: test results aren't persisted yet (run-artifacts #778 unblocks it).

- **Selection v2 — globs, repeated `--select`, `--exclude`, `destination:` selectors** ([#771](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/771)): the dbt-familiar selection vocabulary lands across `drt run` / `drt test` / `drt validate`, which now share one resolver (`drt/cli/_selection.py`) instead of three divergent inline filters (`test`/`validate` were name-only). Grammar per token: bare name with fnmatch globs (`--select 'users_*'`), `tag:<pattern>` (patterns work on tags too), `destination:<type>` (e.g. `destination:hubspot`), and the existing `*`/`all` sentinels. Repeat `--select` to union; `--exclude` subtracts with the same grammar (a select token matching nothing is an error — a typo never silently runs nothing; exclude tokens may match nothing). Definition order is preserved and deduplicated. `--select`/`--exclude` values get best-effort shell completion (sync names, `tag:`s, `destination:`s from the current project). `drt validate` keeps surfacing errors for unparseable syncs selected by name/glob. `source:` from the issue was deliberately dropped: syncs share the project profile, so there is nothing per-sync to select on — revisit if per-sync sources land. Groundwork for `state:modified` (#772) and `drt run --failed` (#773).

### Changed

- **`drt docs generate` — destination labels are docs-safe by default** ([#696](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/696)): the generated site (every page + the shipped `manifest.json`) previously embedded each destination's `describe()` verbatim — full REST endpoint URLs, SMTP hosts, phone numbers, sender emails, `owner/repo`, account ids. Hosting `target/docs/` (the intended use) made that a published inventory of internal endpoints and contact identities. Labels now keep **object identity** (table, channel, sheet, bucket, object type) and drop network locations / personal identifiers: `rest_api (https://internal-host/path)` → `rest_api`, `twilio (+818012345678)` → `twilio (+81…)`, `sendgrid (ops@corp.example)` → `sendgrid (…@corp.example)`, `airtable (appXXX/Leads)` → `airtable (Leads)`; SQL tables, webhooks, buckets, object types are unchanged. **`--full-labels`** restores verbatim output for trusted/internal hosting. Destination node ids (page filenames, manifest names) switch from slug-of-`describe()` — which embedded the URL — to `dest_<slug(safe label)>` with a deterministic `_2`/`_3` suffix on collision: deterministic (byte-identical regen, #697 property preserved), collision-free, and derived from no sensitive value (an earlier hash-of-`describe()` design was rejected in review as brute-forceable for low-entropy values). The seam is a duck-typed `describe_safe()` with a safe-by-default fallback (an unclassified future connector renders type-only), enforced by a completeness + golden-label + planted-sentinel test over the full `DESTINATIONS` registry. Object-store labels keep the per-sync routing `prefix` and drop the bucket/container (public bucket names invite probing); `file`/`parquet` labels keep the file name and drop the directory (absolute paths carry home dirs) — both from @Pawansingh3889's review. The raw-YAML tab's redaction key list also gains `number|sender|recipient|bucket|container|path` (a Twilio `from_number` and an S3 `bucket` slipped through the #702-era list — each caught by the E2E leak scan). MCP's docs manifest flows through the same builder, so it is safe by default too; `drt_get_manifest` gains a `full_labels` opt-in mirroring the CLI flag ([#718](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/718) parity).


- **`drt sources --detailed` now reports `config_class` as `drt.config.profiles.<X>Profile`** ([#721](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/721)): the 13 source profile dataclasses moved from `drt/config/credentials.py` into a new `drt/config/profiles.py` (re-exported from `credentials.py`, so every `from drt.config.credentials import <X>Profile` is unchanged). The `config_class` string in `drt sources --detailed` / `--output json` is built from the class's real module, so it now reads `drt.config.profiles.<X>Profile` instead of `drt.config.credentials.<X>Profile`. The connectors themselves are unchanged — this mirrors the destination-side shift from the earlier `models.py` split ([#721](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/721) phase 1).

- **`drt docs` HTML site — design maturation** (epic [#499](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/499)): the static site (`drt docs generate --format html`, shipped v0.7.11) gains its full visual design and lineage UI, which was the v0.8.0 release gate. A hand-rolled deterministic layout engine emits an inline **static SVG project DAG** with ownership-zone bands (sources · external read → syncs · managed by drt → destinations · external write), replacing runtime Mermaid ([#701](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/701)/[#796](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/796)); a single-`:root` design-token system with light/dark theming, sync/source/destination/tag pages, per-sync ego lineage, connector brand badges, and KPI cards ([#704](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/704)); an **empty/error-state system** ([#751](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/751)); a YAML tab showing each sync **as written on disk** with inline-secret redaction ([#752](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/752)); and an accessibility + finishing pass — skip link, focus rings, print styles, favicon ([#753](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/753)). The whole site is **byte-identical across regenerations** for diffability ([#697](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/697)), runs from `file://` with zero runtime dependencies, and its architecture is recorded in two ADRs under `docs/adr/` ([#500](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/500)).

## [0.7.11] - 2026-07-10

### Added

- **Snowflake key-pair authentication — destination + source (`private_key_env`)** ([#737](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/737), [#745](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/745)): new Snowflake accounts **enforce MFA on password sign-ins**, which breaks programmatic password logins (hit live on the org smoke account — `250001: Multi-factor authentication is required`; the transitional `TYPE = LEGACY_SERVICE` is already unavailable on new accounts). The connector now supports the proper path: a `TYPE = SERVICE` user with an RSA key pair. `private_key_env` (PEM/PKCS#8 contents; optional `private_key_passphrase_env`) on both `SnowflakeDestinationConfig` and the `snowflake` source profile — takes precedence over `password_env`, which becomes optional (one of the two is required; existing password configs keep working unchanged). PEM→DER decoding via `cryptography` (already a snowflake-connector dependency), shared in `drt/config/credentials.py::load_snowflake_private_key`. The DWH smoke harness gains the same dual-auth gate (`DRT_SMOKE_SNOWFLAKE_PRIVATE_KEY` preferred, password fallback; the mirror case followed in [#746](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/746) — **all five Snowflake legs re-validated live under key-pair**) and `docs/connectors/snowflake.md` + the provisioning runbook document the SERVICE-user setup. Periphery aligned in [#748](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/748): the `drt profile add` snowflake spec now prompts for `private_key_env` first (password as fallback) and the LLM docs (`CONTEXT.md` matrix, `API_REFERENCE.md` secrets example) show the key-pair form. Closes [#737](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/737).

- **MCP parity — DLQ/retry, docs manifest, and profile diagnostics as MCP tools** ([#718](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/718)): five new tools close the gap between the MCP surface and the CLI. `drt_dlq` inspects a sync's Dead Letter Queue (depth + queued records) and `drt_retry` replays it (mirrors `drt retry`, v0.7.9) — the whole DLQ capability was previously invisible to agents. `drt_get_manifest` returns the machine-readable sync catalog + lineage (the `drt docs --format json` artifact). `drt_list_profiles` (name + type, secret-free) and `drt_test_profile` (connectivity check) mirror `drt profile`. `drt_run_sync` also gains `cursor_value` (bounded backfill, mirrors `--cursor-value`) and `profile_name` (mirrors `--profile`) overrides. The `drt retry` replay core is extracted into a shared `replay_dead_letters()` so the CLI command and the MCP tool run identical logic. A new parity test asserts `drt_list_connectors` lists exactly the registered connector types (test-time guard against the inventory drift that hid `salesforce_bulk`). 13 new tests in `tests/unit/test_mcp.py`. Closes [#718](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/718) via [#725](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/725).

- **`drt docs generate --format html` — multi-file static docs site** ([#510](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/510), P3 of [#499](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/499)): renders the project manifest into a navigable `target/docs/` site — one HTML page per view (overview, DAG, and each sync / source / destination) plus vendored `assets/` (`style.css`, `app.js`, `pygments-default.css`). Per the ADR ([#500](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/500)): a **multi-file static site** with **no runtime fetch** — each page inlines its data in a `<script type="application/json" id="drt-data">` block, so opening `index.html` via `file://` works with no CORS errors. Hand-written CSS carries the mockup's brand-violet tokens with light/dark via `prefers-color-scheme`; the DAG renders through Mermaid (CDN); sync pages show the catalog entry as Pygments-highlighted YAML (build-time, new `[docs]` extra). `manifest.json` is still emitted (P2 artifact). New `drt/docs/html.py` + `drt/docs/_html_assets.py`; `--format mermaid` / `--format json` unchanged. 8 tests in `tests/unit/test_docs_html.py`. Out of scope (per the issue): `drt docs serve` (P4), vendoring Mermaid, run-history timeline. Closes [#510](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/510). *(moved from [0.7.10]: merged after the v0.7.10 tag, so the published wheel does not contain it — see [#715](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/715).)*

- **Docs-site UI follow-ups — tokens, sync tabs + ego lineage, connector badges, tag pages** ([#704](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/704), post-[#677](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/677)): the generated HTML docs site gains design tokens, per-sync tabbed pages with an ego lineage view, connector badges, and tag index pages.

- **Docs-site DAG layout engine — deterministic ranks, ordering, back-edge lanes** ([#713](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/713), layout core for [#701](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/701)): new `drt/docs/layout.py` — `compute_layout(manifest)` returns pure geometry (node positions + edge paths) for the lineage DAG, with no SVG/styling/JS attached (the emission layer sits on top and consumes it). Ranks are fixed by node kind (source → sync → destination); crossing minimisation offers barycenter *and* median heuristics behind a `strategy=` switch, with fixed iterations + name tiebreaks so output is **byte-identical run to run**; lookup back-edges each get their own lane and a distinct top-edge port on the consuming sync, so N lookups never stack into one curve. Groundwork for replacing the docs site's Mermaid-CDN DAG with a self-contained render.

- **Schema-aware JSON serialization — Databricks STRUCT/ARRAY/MAP/VARIANT** ([#317](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/317), Layer 3, Databricks phase): completes the Databricks leg on top of the `information_schema` introspection foundation. `describe_columns` maps a Databricks table’s complex types (`STRUCT` / `MAP` / `ARRAY` / `VARIANT`) to the `json` category, and the new `describe_databricks_ddls` reads each `STRUCT`/`ARRAY`/`MAP` column’s `full_data_type` (e.g. `array<string>`, `struct<a: int, b: string>`) for the `from_json` schema argument. The destination binds each json-category value per column: `from_json(%s, '<ddl>')` for STRUCT/ARRAY/MAP, `parse_json(%s)` for VARIANT (no DDL form), scalars pass through as `%s`. Because Databricks won’t accept these functions in a `VALUES` clause, wrapping switches the INSERT to the `SELECT` form (`INSERT INTO t (a, v) SELECT %s, from_json(%s, '<ddl>')`), binding `json.dumps(value)` so a `dict`/`list` lands as a proper complex value instead of a stringified `repr`. Applied across all write paths (insert / merge-staging / replace-truncate / replace-swap); when no column needs wrapping the clause is the unchanged `VALUES (%s, …)` — byte-identical to before. The `from_json` DDL is interpolated as a literal read verbatim from `information_schema` (no injection surface); the value stays a bind. `introspect_schema: true` (new field on the Databricks config, default on; opt out for principals without `information_schema` read) with `_resolve_schema` / `_resolve_ddls` caching both maps per sync, mirroring the Snowflake leg. 13 new tests (`tests/unit/test_databricks_schema_aware.py`: value-clause routing incl. case-insensitive match, `json.dumps` encoding round-trip, all four write paths, `introspect_schema: false` opt-out, no-json byte-identical `VALUES`). **Live-validated**: `test_databricks_complex_type_serialization` passed against a real workspace in the [#732](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/732) smoke run (#674 harness), confirming the `from_json` / `parse_json` SELECT-form insert end-to-end. Part of [#317](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/317) (the epic stays open for ClickHouse / BigQuery).

- **DWH smoke harness — full per-warehouse legs + provisioning + cost guard** (wrap-up of the [#654](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/654) epic): the gated/nightly real-warehouse harness ([#674](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/674)) gained its complete coverage — Databricks insert / replace-`INSERT OVERWRITE` / complex-type / connection ([#705](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/705), closes [#672](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/672)), Snowflake replace-swap / VARIANT `PARSE_JSON` complex / connection ([#708](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/708), closes [#671](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/671)), and BigQuery MERGE-via-temp-table + cleanup ([#700](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/700), closes [#673](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/673)). Operational scaffolding around it: reproducible provisioning scripts for the three smoke accounts ([#709](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/709)), a BigQuery billing-account-link gotcha note in the provisioning runbook ([#736](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/736) — the free tier blocks streaming inserts and DML), and a daily DWH cost digest posted to Discord ([#738](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/738), [#710](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/710) Layer 2b; Discord webhook User-Agent fix [#739](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/739), Snowflake key-pair auth for the cost watcher [#741](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/741), digest scheduled 03:20 UTC to reuse the smoke-warmed warehouse [#744](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/744)) so a runaway warehouse bill is caught within a day.

- **DWH smoke: Snowflake mirror + BigQuery complex-type coverage** ([#733](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/733), part of [#654](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/654)): two live-coverage gaps closed in the #674 harness. `test_snowflake_mirror_deletes_unobserved_keys` gives the Snowflake mirror leg ([#340](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/340)) its first real-warehouse check — a pre-seeded stale row the source never emits is deleted while the source rows land (**validated green**). `test_bigquery_complex_type_roundtrip` proves BigQuery's native ARRAY/STRUCT pass-through (no `PARSE_JSON`/`from_json` wrapping) by reading typed sub-fields back — **validated green** once billing was enabled on the smoke project (the first live attempt confirmed BigQuery's free tier blocks streaming inserts and DML). With this, all three DWH legs are live-validated and the [#654](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/654) epic is closed. Test-only, gated on `DRT_SMOKE_*` secrets like the rest of the harness.

### Changed

- **Databricks destination migrated to native `?` paramstyle** ([#707](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/707)): removes the interim `use_inline_params="silent"` opt-in from #706 — all bind sites (`_value_clause` scalar / `from_json` / `parse_json`, and the two schema-introspection queries) now use the connector's default native `?` markers with server-side binding, dropping the per-connection deprecation warning and the escaping-based injection surface inline rendering carried. The `sync.mode: mirror` end-of-sync DELETE is reworked from an inline `NOT IN (?, ?, …)` (one parameter per observed key, which native mode's per-statement parameter limit would cap) to a **staging anti-join**: observed keys are staged into a scratch Delta table and removed via `DELETE … WHERE key NOT IN (SELECT key FROM staging)`, binding no key parameters so it scales to any number of keys. Postgres/MySQL/Snowflake stay pyformat (native to their drivers). **Live-validated** against a real workspace via the #674 smoke harness ([#732](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/732)): insert, replace-swap, complex-type, connection, and mirror all green — the mirror probe staged 300 observed keys (past the native-mode 255-parameter cap that would have broken the old inline `NOT IN`) and deleted the 40 stale rows. The committed smoke keeps a 20-key mirror (the 300-key probe took ~19 min of row-by-row staging — perf follow-up [#734](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/734)); parameter-limit immunity is structural (the DELETE binds zero key parameters) and pinned by the unit suite.

- **Databricks writes batched into multi-row `VALUES` chunks** ([#743](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/743), closes [#734](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/734)): every Databricks write path issued one statement — one warehouse HTTP round trip (~2 s observed) — per row, which is what made the 300-key mirror probe above take ~19 minutes. Scalar-only loads (insert / merge staging / replace) and the mirror key staging now batch into multi-row `VALUES` chunks sized to the native 255-marker limit (e.g. 127 two-column rows per statement — a 127–255× round-trip reduction on typical shapes). Per-row semantics are preserved: a failed chunk lands nothing (a multi-row INSERT is atomic) and is replayed row by row, keeping exact `RowError.batch_index` attribution and `on_error: skip | fail` behaviour; `from_json`/`parse_json` SELECT-form loads (#317) don't compose into multi-row `VALUES` and stay one per row. **Live-validated** on the #674 harness — 5 passed, with the smoke suite itself ~2× faster.

- **README modernised; Japanese i18n retired** ([#712](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/712)): README.ja.md and its translation upkeep are removed; the English README is the single maintained entry point.

- **Destination-layer dedup — shared SQL utilities + dialect-agnostic base** (first tranches of [#719](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/719) / [#722](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/722)): `destinations/sql_utils.py` centralises backtick identifier quoting, row-count dispatch (`RowCountable`), and the mirror capability/upsert-key messages ([#726](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/726)); a dialect-agnostic `BaseSqlDestination` extracts the shared `__init__` / schema resolution / mirror-scope validation / mirror-state accumulation ([#727](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/727)); the mirror-capability guard + `record_preview` helper are shared across SQL destinations ([#730](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/730)); and Snowflake/Databricks `_insert_rows` now requires explicit columns, dropping the dict-ordering `values()` fallback ([#731](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/731)). Related hygiene from [#723](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/723)/[#718](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/718): the CLI's JSON logging moved to `cli/_logging` with source-only connectors documented ([#729](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/729)), and `drt_list_connectors` derives its inventory from the connectors SSoT instead of a hand-kept list ([#728](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/728)). Pure refactors — no behaviour change.

### Fixed

- **Databricks destination: every parameterised write failed on databricks-sql-connector >=3.0** — the destination binds values with pyformat `%s` markers, but connector >=3.0 defaults to *native* paramstyle (`?` / `:name`, server-side binding) and forwards `%s` to the server unexpanded, so every INSERT / merge-staging / replace write died server-side with `[PARSE_SYNTAX_ERROR] Syntax error at or near '%'` (only the parameterless `test_connection` survived). `_connect` now opts back into client-side inline rendering via `use_inline_params="silent"`, restoring all write modes; schema introspection ([#317](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/317)) inherits the fix through the shared `_connect`. Surfaced by running the #674 DWH smoke harness against a real workspace ([#672](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/672)); confirmed broken and re-verified green on connector 3.7.5 and 4.3.0. The Databricks *source* is unaffected (it executes model SQL verbatim, no binds). Migrating the destination to native `?` markers (the connector's forward path — inline params are deprecated upstream) is tracked as a follow-up; note the mirror-mode `NOT IN` delete builds one parameter per key, which needs chunking under native mode's per-statement parameter limit.

- **`drt docs generate --format html` hardening + Databricks DDL-quote safety** ([#703](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/703) via [#711](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/711), follow-up to [#677](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/677) / [#680](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/680)): `render_html` no longer unconditionally deletes `--output` — it refuses a populated directory that isn’t a prior drt-docs build (no `index.html` + `assets/`) and errors if the path is a file, so a mis-pointed `--output` can’t wipe unrelated data (default `target/docs` is unaffected). Missing docs extras now print `pip install drt-core[docs]` instead of a traceback (mirrors `drt mcp run`). Page slugs fail fast on collision — punctuation-only-distinct names (`a_b` vs `a__b`) that map to the same file now raise instead of silently overwriting. And the Databricks `from_json` DDL literal escapes single quotes (`'` → `''`) as defence-in-depth. 6 new tests.

- **Drift audit: multi-line signature blind spot in `check_drift.sh`** ([#714](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/pull/714)): the docs/skills/MCP drift checks matched signatures with single-line regexes, so any declaration wrapped across lines silently escaped auditing — flattening newlines before matching closes the blind spot, and Check 8 now greps for the `connector_inventory` derivation instead of a brittle literal list. Fixing the auditor immediately surfaced (and the same PR closes) hidden `salesforce_bulk` coverage gaps it had been missing.

## [0.7.10] - 2026-07-02

### Added


- **Delta Lake + Apache Iceberg sources** ([#172](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/172), [#173](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/173)): read lakehouse tables from local / S3 / GCS as a drt source. `type: deltalake` (via delta-rs) and `type: iceberg` (via pyiceberg) load the table into Arrow, register it in an in-memory DuckDB, and run your model SQL against it — so column selection and incremental cursor filters work like any other source. Optional extras `drt-core[deltalake]` / `drt-core[iceberg]`; cloud auth via `storage_options` (Delta) / catalog `properties` (Iceberg) with `*_ENV` env-var resolution. New `docs/connectors/deltalake.md` + `iceberg.md`. Closes [#172](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/172), [#173](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/173).

- **PII masking before load — `sync.mask`** ([#427](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/427)): hash or redact named fields after extraction and just before the destination, without touching the source SQL. New `mask: {field: hash | redact}` on `SyncOptions` (flat per the v1 design — `hash` = SHA-256 hex digest, `redact` = `[REDACTED]`), applied as a pure transform in `drt/engine/masking.py` at the same seam as `field_mappings` (after the rename, so keys reference the destination-facing name) and therefore outside the `engine/sync.py` SyncObserver boundary. Null values pass through untouched and non-strings are stringified before hashing; a configured field absent from a row is skipped; the input batch is never mutated. Works across every destination. Param-bearing strategies (e.g. `truncate`) are a backwards-compatible follow-up ([#660](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/660)). 15 unit tests in `tests/unit/test_masking.py` (config validation including rejecting an unknown strategy, hash determinism, redact, null passthrough, non-string stringify, absent-field skip, no-mutation). Closes [#427](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/427).

- **PII masking — `truncate` strategy + object config form** ([#660](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/660), follow-up to [#427](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/427)): adds a `truncate` strategy that keeps the first `length` characters of a value (e.g. `length: 2` → initials), introduced via a backwards-compatible **object form** `{strategy: truncate, length: N}` on `sync.mask`. The flat form (`field: hash | redact`) is unchanged; the object form is validated so `truncate` requires a non-negative `length` while the parameter-less strategies reject `length`. New `MaskRule` model and `MaskSpec` union in `drt/config/models.py`; the engine transform handles both forms at the same pure `field_mappings` seam, preserving null passthrough and non-string stringify. Regenerated the bundled VS Code `sync.schema.json` to expose the object form. `tests/unit/test_masking.py` extended — object-form acceptance and validation (truncate-needs-length, `length` on a parameter-less strategy rejected, unknown strategy rejected) plus `truncate` behaviour (first-N, `length: 0`, length ≥ value length, non-string, null). Closes [#660](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/660).

- **Schema-aware JSON serialization — Snowflake VARIANT/OBJECT/ARRAY** ([#317](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/317), Layer 3, Phase 2a): extends the introspection machinery to Snowflake. `describe_columns` now reads Snowflake's `INFORMATION_SCHEMA.COLUMNS` (per-database, matched case-insensitively so quoted and unquoted identifiers both resolve) and maps the semi-structured types — `VARIANT` / `OBJECT` / `ARRAY` — to the `json` category. Because Snowflake **disallows functions in a `VALUES` clause**, those columns can't be bound directly; the destination switches the INSERT to the `SELECT` form and wraps them with `PARSE_JSON` (`INSERT INTO t (a, v) SELECT %s, PARSE_JSON(%s)`), binding `json.dumps(value)` so a `dict`/`list` lands as proper semi-structured data instead of a stringified `repr`. Applies across all write paths (insert / merge-staging / replace-truncate / replace-swap); when no column needs wrapping the clause is the unchanged `VALUES (%s, …)` — byte-identical to before. `introspect_schema: true` (new field, default on; opt out for roles without `information_schema` read) and `_resolve_schema`'s per-sync cache mirror the Postgres/MySQL Phase-1 design. 9 new tests (`tests/unit/test_schema.py` Snowflake introspection: type→category, db-scoped case-insensitive query, empty/failure → `None`; `tests/unit/test_snowflake_destination.py` load integration: VARIANT → `SELECT`/`PARSE_JSON` + `json.dumps`, all-scalar → unchanged `VALUES`, per-sync cache, opt-out). **Note for reviewer/real-account validation**: the `SELECT … PARSE_JSON(%s)` insert form is the canonical Snowflake VARIANT-load pattern but the suite is mock-based (like the rest of the Snowflake destination) — worth a sanity check against a live warehouse before release. **ClickHouse / BigQuery** introspection deferred — verified no serialization gap (clickhouse-connect is schema-aware; the BigQuery client is JSON-native), so no speculative wiring; Databricks tracked as a later phase.


- **Scoped mirror — restrict differential deletes to observed parents (`sync.mirror.scope`)** ([#687](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/687)): the stateless fit for **1:N regeneration** — a parent entity periodically regenerated together with its child link rows (`parent_id`, `child_id`). Plain `mode: full` accumulates stale children forever (the motivating production case measured 54 of 55 previously-synced link rows stale); #340 mirror diffs the whole table and would delete rows under parents the sync never saw. With `scope: [parent_id]`, the mirror DELETE becomes `WHERE parent_id IN (observed parents) AND upsert_key NOT IN (observed keys)` — stale children under observed parents go, rows under unobserved parents are untouched. Stateless, idempotent, self-healing (every run recomputes the diff within the observed scope). Scope values are accumulated with the same successful-rows filter as the mirror key set; a scope column missing from the model output **fails fast before any write** (runtime check at first batch — config-time can't see model output); composite scopes supported; the #340 empty-source guard is unchanged. `scope` + `strategy: tracked` (#686) is rejected for now — composition is a follow-up. **Postgres + MySQL**; ClickHouse / Snowflake / Databricks fail fast. 10 new tests. Closes [#687](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/687).

- **Tracked mirror — state-based differential deletes (`sync.mirror.strategy: tracked`)** ([#686](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/686)): makes `sync.mode: mirror` safe on destination tables the **application also writes to** — the canonical Reverse ETL destination. The #340 mirror diffs against the whole destination table, so it deletes co-writer rows; users with shared tables were stuck on `mode: full` and accumulated stale rows forever. `strategy: tracked` adopts the Census/Hightouch semantics instead: drt persists the set of `upsert_key` tuples **it has itself synced** in a drt-managed `_drt_synced_keys` side table in the destination (`sync_name` / `key_hash` / `key_json`, created lazily in the target's schema — durable across ephemeral runners), and each run deletes only `previously-synced − current-source` keys, then rewrites the state **in the same transaction** as the delete. First run baselines without deleting; lost state re-baselines with a WARN; the #340 empty-source and failed-rows guards carry over (a transient empty source keeps the baseline; only successfully-written keys enter the state). New `MirrorConfig` on `SyncOptions` (`mirror:` rejected outside `mode: mirror`), sync name plumbed via a `SyncConfig`-injected private attr rather than a YAML field or a Destination-protocol change. **Postgres + MySQL** in this phase (same phasing as #317); ClickHouse / Snowflake / Databricks fail fast with a clear error instead of silently falling back to the co-writer-unsafe destination diff. int/str keys round-trip exactly in the state table; non-JSON-native key types are stringified (documented limitation). 20 new tests across `tests/unit/test_config.py`, `test_mirror_state.py`, `test_postgres_mirror_mode.py`, `test_mysql_mirror_mode.py` and the three guard suites. `--dry-run --diff` preview of tracked deletions and the DWH-destination legs are tracked as follow-ups. Closes [#686](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/686).

- **Schema-aware JSON serialization — Postgres & MySQL (`introspect_schema`)** ([#317](https://github.com/drt-hub/drt/issues/317), Layer 3, Phase 1): drt now reads `INFORMATION_SCHEMA.COLUMNS` for the target table **once per sync** and routes each `dict` / `list` value by the column's real type — JSON/JSONB columns get JSON-encoded (dict **and** list), native `ARRAY` columns pass the list through to the driver's adapter. This resolves the **list→JSONB-vs-ARRAY ambiguity** that `json_columns` alone can't (a bare Python `list` could mean either) and removes the need to declare `json_columns` at all for the common case. **Precedence**: an explicit `json_columns` allowlist (Layer 2, #316) always wins; otherwise introspection decides; otherwise the pre-existing "encode every complex value" behaviour applies. **Best-effort and backward-compatible**: a locked-down `information_schema`, a missing driver, or a not-yet-created table makes introspection return nothing and the sync keeps its prior behaviour — and the shared serializer with `schema=None` is byte-for-byte identical to before. On by default; set `introspect_schema: false` (new field on the shared SQL destination config) for permission-locked environments.
